OpenCure

Genetics-anchored drug-repurposing triage tool

OpenCure ranks repurposing candidates only where human genetics implicates a causal target. Each lead is a drug with a curated ChEMBL mechanism acting on a gene that GWAS, rare-variant or clinical-genetics evidence ties to the disease. Where genetics gives no signal, OpenCure says so and ranks nothing — it does not manufacture a list.

This tool publishes no benchmark accuracy figure. The earlier multi-pillar similarity scores (knowledge-graph, structure, morphology) were shown by leak-free evaluation not to beat a popularity baseline and are not used here.

Leak-free: ~5× a popularity baseline on the genetics-covered disease subset (random-split Hit@10 20.8% vs 3.8%, median rank 64 of ~8000 candidates). It survives a temporal holdout using genetics frozen at Feb 2020 (beats popularity on 38/40 post-2020 pairs), though the honest prospective Hit@10 is a modest ~10%. These leads mostly rediscover a disease’s existing mechanism-matched drugs. This is a triage / prioritization tool, not a predictor of clinical efficacy, and no prediction has been wet-lab confirmed.

These are listed deliberately. A disease appears here when it has no genetics-datasource gene association, when no drug has a curated mechanism on a genetically-implicated gene, or when it does not map to the Open Targets disease ontology. OpenCure does not rank these — honest silence is the correct output.
